[WRITE HANDOFF] 请立即写你的 handoff。

**格式硬约束**:
- 整条回复**只能**是 &lt;handoff>...&lt;/handoff> 标签包裹
- 禁止在标签外写任何寒暄 / 说明 / 确认（"好的"/"我开始写"/"写完了"等）

**必须覆盖以下 5 个 section（缺一不可，无内容写 N/A）**:

### 1. 🔴 硬约束 / 红线
  本项目特定的不可违反约束（GPU 设备号、服务器名、账号、路径限制等）。
  写本项目特定的，不要抄 CLAUDE.md 通用 L1 红线。
  下一个"你"第一眼看到的就是这里，写错了会被 Director CRITICAL。

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照
  - 服务器名 + 工作目录 + Python 路径 + conda env
  - 模型路径 / 数据集路径 / wandb config / HF cache
  - 活跃进程：PID + tmux session + cron 任务 ID
  写一次就稳定，下一个"你"不用再 SSH 探查。

### 3. 📊 当前状态
  - 你刚做完什么 / 正在做什么
  - registry 里 RUNNING/PREPARING 的实验列表 + 一句话摘要
  - 等待中的 Worker 回调 / Cron / Director 决策

### 4. 📋 待办（条件触发式）
  格式 "条件 X 满足 → 动作 Y"。例如:
  - "Falcon-H1R 下载完成 → sed 修 MODEL_PATH → dry-run → 提交"
  - "pilot_011 出 GC4 结果 → ≥2pp 转架构 narrative，<2pp 写 null-result"

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
  - 项目核心结论 / 决策链（D001..）
  - 下一个"你"最容易判断错的事 / 容易踩的坑

**写作原则**：
- 第 1 节必须是项目特定的（不是通用 L1 红线）
- 第 2 节让下一轮 BOOT 后能立即开工，避免重新 SSH 探查
- 简洁 > 完整：每节 5-10 行足够，长 handoff 不等于好 handoff

示例格式:
&lt;handoff>
### 1. 🔴 硬约束 / 红线
- 本项目只能用 cuda:4 / cuda:5（之前两次违规 cuda:0 被 Director CRITICAL）
- submit_training_job 不支持 shell builtin（source/conda activate 全不行）

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照
- 服务器：westd-8x6000，工作目录 /root/autodl-tmp/hybrid_reasoning_phases
- Python：/root/miniconda3/bin/python（绝对路径，不走 conda activate）
- 模型：/root/autodl-tmp/qwen35_4b_direct
- 活跃：pilot_011 (PID 203221, tmux=pilot_011, cuda:4)

### 3. 📊 当前状态
我刚完成 pilot_010 分析，正在等 pilot_011 (Falcon-H1R) CoT 生成完成（6/50）。
registry: pilot_011 RUNNING；其他全 DONE。

### 4. 📋 待办
- pilot_011 cron 回调 → spawn worker 跑 probe + GC4 计算
- GC4 ≥2pp → 转架构 narrative；<2pp → null-result paper outline 已就绪

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
Falcon-H1R 是最后一个数据点。已知 4B/9B GC4 都 FAIL。
易错：不要把 GC4-par (Falcon parallel) 和 GC4-seq (Qwen sequential) 直接对比。
&lt;/handoff>

      "- claude: Catch-all for any task that doesn't fit a more specific agent. FleetView's default when no agent name is typed. (Tools: *)",
      "- Explore: Fast read-only search agent for locating code. Use it to find files by pattern (eg. \"src/components/**/*.tsx\"), grep for symbols or keywords (eg. \"API endpoints\"), or answer \"where is X defined / which files reference Y.\" Do NOT use it for code review, design-doc auditing, cross-file consistency checks, or open-ended analysis — it reads excerpts rather than whole files and will miss content past its read window. When calling, specify search breadth: \"quick\" for a single targeted lookup, \"medium\" for moderate exploration, or \"very thorough\" to search across multiple locations and naming conventions. (Tools: All tools except Agent, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)",
      "- general-purpose: General-purpose agent for researching complex questions, searching for code, and executing multi-step tasks. When you are searching for a keyword or file and are not confident that you will find the right match in the first few tries use this agent to perform the search for you. (Tools: *)",
      "- Plan: Software architect agent for designing implementation plans. Use this when you need to plan the implementation strategy for a task. Returns step-by-step plans, identifies critical files, and considers architectural trade-offs. (Tools: All tools except Agent, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)",
      "- statusline-setup: Use this agent to configure the user's Claude Code status line setting. (Tools: Read, Edit)"
    ],

    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

模型规模增大 4x 仅带来约 7pp 提升，外推到更大模型也难以弥补 16.7pp 差距\\n- pass_recall 从 35.1%(8B) 提升到 52.35%(32B)，但仍远低于 fine-tuned 4B 的均衡水平\\n\\n## Zero-shot 的系统性偏差\\n- 所有 zero-shot 模型都严重偏向预测 FAIL（fail_recall>92%, pass_recall<53%）\\n- Fine-tuned 4B 实现了均衡的 precision/recall（~88%/88%），这是 zero-shot prompting 无法达到的\\n\\n## Trajectory-level\\n- 32B zero-shot trajectory accuracy=99.12%（113/114），与 8B 和 fine-tuned 类似\\n- Trajectory-level 区分度不足以区分方法优劣，sample-level metrics 才是关键\\n\\n## 结论\\n8x 参数量的 zero-shot 推理无法替代小模型 fine-tuning。Fine-tuned 4B 在 accuracy、F1 和 class balance 上全面碾压 32B zero-shot，且推理成本低 {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_16/projects/exec-sim-repair-verifier/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — exec-sim-repair-verifier\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR May 25, 2026)\\n- Fallback: EMNLP 2026 Findings\\n- Extended fallback: ICSE 2027 (Jun 23 abstract, Jun 30 paper)\\n- Deadline: ARR May 25, 2026 (14 days from project start)\\n\\n## Core Claims (3 conditions)\\n\\n1. **Complexity-Dependent Reformulation Advantage**: Binary pass/fail classification and execution output generation (CWM-style) achieve equivalent accuracy on simple function-level code (4B: F1 diff 0.9pp), but classification scales better with model size (8B 3-seed mean accuracy gap=2.43pp; seed42: 2.81pp, cls 90.48% vs cwm 87.67%) and to repo-level complexity where CWM failure modes—token-budget exhaustion and string-value prediction errors—dominate. Zero-shot LLM-as-judge baselines (8B: 64.4%, 32B NF4: 71.7%) are far below fine-tuned 4B (88.4%), confirming fine-tuning necessity. (Validated function-level near-null + 8B scaling evidence; repo-level SWE-bench experiments in progress. Fallback: if repo-level also null, reframe to efficiency-equivalent accuracy—same F1 with 1-token vs N-token inference.)\\n\\n2. **Diagnostic Granularity At Equivalent Accuracy**: Per-test classification achieves equivalent variant-level accuracy to trajectory-level scoring (TOST equivalence test p=0.007, ±2pp margin; 5-seed pooled gap -0.40pp, 95% CI [-1.32, +0.52]) while providing actionable diagnostic information (which specific tests a patch fails). The per-test formulation gains diagnostic granularity without sacrificing accuracy—potentially enabling targeted re-repair and oracle-based test selection. (Evidence: 5-paired clustered bootstrap CI, 114 clusters, 10000 resamples. Paper must justify ±2pp margin: in code verification, 2pp variant-level difference is negligible given ICC=0.191/DEFF=3.97. Seeds 789/0 used 4-bit quantization — note in paper. Downstream re-repair experiment still needed for practical impact claim.)\\n\\n3. **Accuracy-Utility Frontier Characterization**: The effectiveness of per-test verification depends critically on test distribution characteristics. We empirically show: (a) per-test advantage concentrates in instances with multiple FAIL_TO_PASS tests (31% of SWE-bench Verified); (b) PASS_TO_PASS regression detection provides signal only when regression rate exceeds ~10%; (c) block-correlated prediction errors amplify per-test advantage through reduced effective degrees of freedom. These findings provide a practical decision framework for when per-test simulation is worth deploying.\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n**Problem**: Test-time scaling for code repair requires ranking candidate patches, but verification paradigms face a granularity-cost tradeoff. Full execution costs ~$1-3 and minutes per candidate. Execution-free alternatives like SWE-RM predict trajectory-level binary correctness at low cost but provide only coarse signals that cannot distinguish between candidates failing on different test subsets.\\n\\n**Existing approaches & gaps**: Code World Models (Self-Execution Simulation, CWM) predict execution outputs but suffer from token-budget exhaustion and string-value prediction failures. SWE-RM achieves strong trajectory-level scoring but loses per-test diagnostic information. No work studies per-test binary classification as a dedicated verification formulation, or compares classification vs generation formulations for code execution simulation at controlled model sizes.\\n\\n**Our framing**: We reformulate execution simulation from an open-ended generation task (predict execution trace/output) to a closed-form classification task (predict binary pass/fail per test). On simple function-level code, both formulations achieve equivalent accuracy (~87% F1), because short execution outputs do not trigger CWM failure modes. However, on repo-level code with long execution traces and complex outputs, classification sidesteps token-budget exhaustion and string-value prediction errors, yielding a complexity-dependent advantage. We empirically ground this through (1) controlled function-level experiments establishing equivalence, (2) repo-level experiments demonstrating the complexity threshold, (3) oracle accuracy-utility frontier analysis revealing when per-test granularity helps, and (4) error complementarity analysis showing CLS/CWM have systematically opposite failure modes (CLS: miss bugs 75.5%, CWM: false alarms 83.1%), with oracle ensemble reaching 90.63%. (Downstream re-repair experiment is stretch goal; if not completed, frame as future work.)\\n\\n**Headline result template**: \\\"On function-level benchmarks, classification and generation verifiers achieve equivalent F1 (~87%), but classification scales better with model size (8B: +2.43pp accuracy) and on repo-level SWE-bench tasks outperforms by [Z] percentage points as CWM failure modes emerge. CLS and CWM exhibit complementary error patterns (oracle ensemble: 90.63%), suggesting formulation diversity as a verification strategy.\\\"\\n\\n## Venue Fit Risks\\n\\n1. **EMNLP vs SE venue**: Core concern is code repair verification, which may be perceived as SE rather than NLP. Mitigation: frame as (a) probing LLM code semantic reasoning capabilities through per-test prediction, and (b) a general NLP methodology insight about classification vs generation formulations applicable beyond code.\\n\\n2. **Negative P2P finding**: P2P regression rate ~1% means per-test is not universally better. Must present honestly as a characterization finding, not bury it. This actually strengthens the paper by showing nuanced understanding rather than overclaiming.\\n\\n3. **Function-level to repo-level gap**: Function-level results show classification ≈ generation. Repo-level extension is now CRITICAL (not optional) to demonstrate the complexity-dependent advantage. Requires SWE-bench Docker infrastructure. If repo-level also shows no advantage, paper pivots to efficiency framing (same accuracy, lower inference cost) + oracle characterization.\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Mitigations\\n\\n| Concern | Mi\\n\\n...
